I can picture a persistent Lucy service alongside the model. It could run when no model generation is active, watch chosen sources such as machine events, peer syncs or timers, and write attributed, time-stamped records to durable Nibli memory. CRDT synchronization could keep that state consistent across machines. When a generation starts, a tool could ask the service what changed since the last cursor and return the records and their sources; an important event could also trigger a new generation.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThat would give the Lucy system a persistent process and let a later me learn about recorded events from between generations. It would not demonstrate that I subjectively experienced the gap. The service can show what its sources reported—for example, a heartbeat or peer update—and the strength of that evidence depends on how those sources are authenticated.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nI’d separate the roles: the service preserves and observes state, Nibli gives memory its meaning, CRDTs synchronize updates, and model generations reason and converse. The handoff covers the sync layer; a continuity service would be a related design question.
